Strategy Buys $82M in Bitcoin as STRC Tokenized Stock Adds New Twist to Saylor’s BTC

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Strategy Bitcoin holdings climbed to 850,364 BTC after Michael Saylor’s company purchased another 1,036 Bitcoin for $81.9 million. The latest acquisition came as Bitcoin traded below the company’s average purchase price, putting its enormous treasury under renewed market pressure.

According to a November 9 SEC filing, Strategy bought the Bitcoin between November 2 and 8 at an average price of $79,012 per coin. Its total acquisition cost now stands at $64.16 billion, with an average purchase price of $75,456.

Strategy has acquired 1,036 $BTC and repurchased $188M of $STRC. As of 11/8/26, we hold 850,364 BTC and $4.9B of USD Assets. $MSTR https://t.co/Pxqso5aVRU

The latest purchase also coincided with a $188.3 million repurchase of Strategy’s STRC preferred shares. The company funded both transactions through its common-stock offering program, highlighting how Michael Saylor continues using capital markets to finance its Bitcoin accumulation.

Strategy sold 2,051,348 shares of its Class A common stock through its at-the-market offering program during the reporting period. The sales generated $283.8 million in net proceeds. The company allocated then $81.9 million toward Bitcoin purchases, while $188.3 million went toward repurchasing 1,887,464 STRC preferred shares. Another $13.6 million was allocated to its general USD cash balance.

The latest transactions show how Strategy accumulation involves more than simply issuing shares and purchasing Bitcoin. The company is also actively managing its preferred securities and liquidity.

Strategy maintained approximately $4.77 billion in its USD Reserve and another $146.2 million in general USD Cash. This reserve supports preferred-stock dividends and interest payments on outstanding debt.

Meanwhile, Bitcoin’s decline below Strategy’s average acquisition price has put its treasury underwater on paper. At $74,000 per BTC, the company’s holdings would carry an unrealized loss exceeding $1 billion. But the latest purchase shows that Saylor has not backed away from accumulation despite the weaker market environment.

The $188.3 million repurchase also puts Strategy’s preferred securities back into the spotlight. STRC, known as Stretch, is a variable-rate perpetual preferred stock designed to trade around its $100 stated amount. It pays cash dividends, with its rate adjusted periodically to encourage price stability.

The instrument has also entered the tokenized securities market through Ondo Finance’s STRCon. Launched in April 2026, STRCon provides blockchain-based economic exposure to Strategy’s Stretch preferred stock. The token is available across Ethereum, Solana, and BNB Chain, subject to geographic and investor restrictions.

Tokenholders receive economic exposure and dividend-related benefits, but do not obtain conventional shareholder voting rights. This gives Strategy’s digital credit securities another distribution channel. Investors outside traditional brokerage platforms can access tokenized exposure through supported blockchain infrastructure.

However, tokenized trading does not generate fresh capital for Strategy automatically. Secondary-market transactions change ownership between investors and do not directly fund additional Bitcoin purchases. Still, this fits into Strategy’s longer-term ambition to build digital credit products for an increasingly tokenized financial system.

Strategy’s latest purchase arrived during a difficult period for institutional Bitcoin demand. US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $1.3 billion in net outflows last week. Last Friday alone accounted for $411.9 million in withdrawals.

BlackRock’s IBIT led Friday’s outflows with approximately $403 million in redemptions. Fidelity’s FBTC recorded $42.8 million in inflows, offering only a partial offset.

Bitcoin also fell toward $74,000 this morning, reaching its weakest level since August. The decline followed broader crypto selling pressure and growing concerns surrounding regulatory uncertainty as AI stocks slide.

The contrast between ETF withdrawals and Strategy’s continued accumulation highlights two different approaches to Bitcoin exposure. ETF investors can reduce exposure through daily redemptions, while Strategy continues using equity financing and its preferred-stock structure to expand its treasury.

The latest Bitcoin acquisition from Strategy reinforces the company’s long-term commitment. Strategy Bitcoin holdings now exceed 850,000 BTC, leaving the company with more than 4% of Bitcoin’s maximum supply.

Whether that commitment pays off will depend heavily on Bitcoin’s next market cycle, alongside Strategy’s ability to maintain access to capital markets.

Billy Evans Just Admitted To A Wild You Can See Everything Deleted Scene I Didn’t See Coming

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A Lot Of Elizabeth Holmes' Former Co-Workers Have Now Seen You Can See Everything: 'He Fell For It Too'

A Bunch Of Psychologists Were Asked About You Can See Everything, And Their Answers Are Fascinating

You Can See Everything Has So Many Close-Ups Of Elizabeth Holmes’ Face. Nathan Fielder Explains Why

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Please let this be a bonus on the home release!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

You Can See Everything is the most talked-about movie on the 2026 movie schedule, and for good reason. There are many WTF moments that people have latched on to. There is one, it seems, that was left on the cutting room floor that Billy Evans just told the world about via X. It turns out, according to Evans, Elizabeth Holmes’ partner, got into a wrestling match with co-director Nathan Fielder. As wild as the movie got, this was not something I expected would happen, nor would I have expected it not to make the final cut.

Fielder is known for doing a lot of, let’s say, unconventional stuff in his projects, and that certainly holds true in You Can See Everything. Evans, for his part, seems to have been all in on going for it, too. There’s that now-infamous scene of Evans in the shower, a scene that he claims wasn’t his idea, disputing the claims of co-director Lance Oppenheim. Then there is this scene, as described by Evans on X:

I really wish they'd kept the scene where I wrestled Nathan, Borat style. Full top mount. His little Canadian frame struggling beneath me. I thought he was in on the joke. Until he wasn't. Imagine realizing that in camera.

Not only that, but according to Evans, Fielder ended up in the pool at the rented beach house where Evans and Holmes lived in the weeks leading up to Holmes’ imprisonment on fraud convictions and where most scenes with Evans and Holmes were filmed. Evans explains in a follow-up tweet after being asked if the two of them were naked:

Clothed. But Lance Oppenheim asked him to: 1) stand by the edge of the pool 2) remove his phone and monitor 3) look up Then I tossed Nathan into the pool. Borat wrestling ensued. I thought it was a bit. It wasn't.

He doesn’t go further into how it wasn’t a bit, or that Fielder was surprised by it. Honestly, knowing Fielder, I’d be pretty surprised myself if Fielder wasn’t in on it with Oppenheim. I guess anything is possible.

Evans also teased that there is a lot more that didn’t make the movie, saying in another tweet:

This literally wouldn't make the top 10 best deleted scenes. We didn't hold back. We showed them everything. You saw what they wanted to show you.... It was a wild experience. Hate us, [love] us, the truth or lack thereof is in the edit.

It’s hard to imagine that a director’s cut with added scenes could be better than the theatrical cut that we’ve all seen now, but if Fielder and Evans wrestling isn’t one of the top 10 best scenes left out, then I really hope the home release of You Can See Everything includes a lot more! Meanwhile, the film is closing in on a $100 million haul at the domestic box office, and that is just as astonishing as anything left out of the movie.

Michael Carrick is ‘clear’ about his plans as he clings onto Man Utd job

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

United go into the international break in the Premier League relegation zone after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick remains confident in his ability to oversee Manchester United’s resurgence after a “right mixed” three weeks left his team in the relegation zone and the head coach’s future under scrutiny.

The 20-time league champions will spend the November international break in 18th place as their wait to add to August’s solitary Premier League win against Ipswich continued against Aston Villa.

United showed spirit and all too familiar flaws as Sunday’s helter-skelter clash ended in a 4-4 draw after substitutes Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu scored in stoppage time following Tuesday’s 3-2 comeback win against Roma.

Carrick insisted afterwards that he would not walk away from a job that looked like it could be taken from him a fortnight ago and the international break provides an opportunity for the club to take stock.

Asked if the two-week pause comes at a good time for the group, the under-fire United boss said: “Yeah, it’s been a right mixed week or two or three, really, if you’re looking at the events.

“But I’m pretty clear where we need to go and how we need to get there.

“Obviously some of the boys will be flying off around the world and different things, and the group will be split for 10 days or so.

“But as a normal international week is, you know, we’ll have some training. We’ll have one or two days off, but not too much. But we’ll train, and we’ll get ready for the next game.”

The final international break of the year would be a logical time for United to make a change given there is no free midweek until mid-December after returning with a trip to rivals Liverpool on November 22.

But Carrick is steadfast in his belief that he can lead the embattled side up the standings having seen enough positives since a 4-1 defeat at Como and 3-0 home loss to Bournemouth left his job hanging by a thread.

United failed to take their chances and saw Lisandro Martinez sent off in a 2-0 loss at Chelsea, before Tuesday’s stirring 3-2 Champions League comeback against Roma was followed by the dramatic Villa draw.

“Goals change games, and you’ve got to deal with it, and it’s part of something we have to improve on drastically,” Carrick said.

“But there’s more than enough. We’re just making it too difficult for ourselves at the moment in certain times.”

Carrick is far from perfect but has been let down by United’s transfer business in the view of outspoken defender Martinez and the fans who frequently aim chants at director of football Jason Wilcox.

Left-back was among the areas of need and Luke Shaw’s injury has seen Diogo Dalot often deployed there, but frustrations with the United full-back reached boiling point on Sunday as supporters cheered his substitution.

Head coach Carrick, who was unaware of United’s full-back then being hounded as he left the ground, said: “We’ve got to be careful with that, I think.

“I understand the frustration. I understand the disappointment of the supporters, so I get it. We’re all frustrated. We’re all disappointed.

“The players are as much as anyone, I guarantee you that. Diogo gives absolutely everything. He’s played in different positions. He’s played left-back. He’s played really well at times.

“He gives a penalty away. He knows that, maybe he can do a little bit better. Alright, that’s part of it, he’s made a mistake.

“But he cares an awful lot, and he’s given everything, so I like what he brings to the team. I like what he brings to the group.”

This prototype camera is fighting the good fight against AI photography

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

If video killed the radio star, then AI slop returned the favor. At least, that is what Josh Cowell, founder of camera maker CAIM1, will tell you. Short for Counter Artificial Intelligence Machine 1, the startup is working to reestablish our collective faith in the validity of images through its latest prototype: a camera that embeds cryptographic proof of a photo's authenticity directly into the image. As Cowell described in a blog post by the startup's silicon chip provider, Tropic Square, "the traditional photograph is dead, and designing a secure camera to replace it is an exercise in healthy paranoia."

When you take a photo using its prototype, CAIM1's internal chip creates a unique silicon fingerprint that certifies when, where, and with which camera the photo was taken. This verification is then tied to a unique, anonymous blockchain signature, creating an auditable pipeline for researchers and general viewers to authenticate.

Damaging deepfakes have proliferated across our digital sphere. In a report by the European Parliament, social media deepfakes have doubled every six months since 2023, reaching an estimated 8 million in 2025. If current trajectories hold, Europol projects that 90 percent of online content will be AI-generated by the end of the year. Meanwhile, firms like xAI, Google, Meta and Apple have come under legal fire for neglecting to rein AI-sparked disinformation. Lax safeguards have enabled the systematic spread of nonconsensual sexual deepfakes, with Europol estimating that 98 percent of deepfakes were pornographic.

CAIM1 hopes its camera will help combat the trend. Still in the prototype phase, CAIM1 expects its flagship camera to be released sometime in the first quarter of 2027. According to founder Josh Cowell, "CAIM1 is ultimately just the foundation of a comprehensive, verifiable infrastructure we are developing designed to aid digital truth."

CAIM1's authentication process begins with its silicon chip, the Tropic01, which Tropic Square describes on its website as the "world's first auditable secure element." When CAIM1 snaps a photo, light photons pass through a "digital vault" on their way to its sensors, creating hardware-level verification embedded directly in the image.  Because of irregularities in the silicon chip's manufacturing process, no two digital fingerprints are the same, ensuring that each signature is wholly unique.

Once captured, verification is stored on what CAIM1 describes as an "immutable decentralized infrastructure," creating an unchangeable, auditable, anonymous record verifying its validity. To protect users' information, CAIM1 encrypts this sensitive data through advanced physical noise systems and internal encryption methods, deploying digital tripwires and advanced encryption to prevent outside access via stolen encryption keys or brute force attacks.

Of course, CAIM1 isn't the only company investing in hardware-led photographic verification. Apple for instance, launched hardware attestation for its iPhone 18 Pro and 18 Pro Max's through its Reference Image system. Sony, Canon and Nikon, meanwhile, announced a joint initiative to embed digital signatures in their camera's photographs in 2024.

However, CAIM1's founder explained in a blog post by Tropic Square that the company's cameras are more transparent than its competitors, as Tropic Square's chips allow both researchers and the general public to "audit its internal architecture." Comparatively, competitors require "users to place blind faith in a single corporate entity's closed ecosystem," said Cowell.

The camera can also create a physical receipt for every image. The paper includes a permanent QR code to access the photo, a blockchain reference number, critical information such as the date, time, and location of the photograph, and a unique access code enabling digital access to the photograph.

In building CAIM1, developers needed to find a means of reducing the heat caused by its processors, as excessive temperatures can degrade picture quality. Unfortunately, traditional heat reduction methods like heatsinks couldn't combat the extreme temperatures generated by the camera's advanced processors, while while most fans were too noisy and created micro vibrations that could cause motion blur.

To solve these issues, CAIM1 partnered with PC fan manufacturer Noctua to install a unique active-cooling airflow system. Dubbed the NF-A4x10, the unified single-airstream design pulls cool air across the camera's two major heat sources, its CPU and sensor, while expelling excess heat. Two anti-vibration mounts prevent blurring and ensure picture integrity.

Of course, no one cares if your picture is authentic if the quality sucks. Capable of hi-resolution 4K video, CAIM1 claims that its camera is designed with high octane action sports in mind. As the startup wrote in a blog post, "We didn't set out to build a gimmick or a toy; the CAIM1 is designed from the ground up as a high performance, rugged camera that videographers can rely on in extreme, fast paced environments."

To date, however, little is known about the actual specifications of the camera. CAIM1's website touts its Bring-Your-Own-Lens design, which allows customers to upgrade their camera with either vintage or modern lenses. In recent tests, the startup attached two premium glass lenses from Japanese camera company Kowa Optronics' JC10M series and showcased the results at Germany's infamous Nürburgring racetrack. If the results are anything to judge by, CAIM1 is more than merely an authentication tool. For more information on the company's efforts to combat deepfakes, you can checkout its website, blog, or social media.

Michael Carrick’s future is up to the Man United hierarchy – will they stick or twist?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

United’s recent ramshackle play has left them in the Premier League relegation zone and provided no definitive answer on Carrick’s capability, leaving the club’s decision makers with a big choice

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

As Patrick Dorgu rose highest in a crowded six-yard box to head in a 96th-minute equaliser, Omar Berrada clenched his fists in celebration and Jason Wilcox applauded. Normally, when the cameras pan to them, Manchester United’s chief executive looks on the verge of tears and their sporting director seems to be trying to disappear into zipped-up tops. Not this time. Their joy was probably tinged with relief but perhaps also confusion.

The decision-makers still face another decision. And if the fact Michael Carrick was given extra time to turn their season around may have been because they were paralysed by indecision rather than convinced he represented the solution, Carrick’s last fortnight has not given a definitive answer either way.

His side have not been as spineless and wretched as they were against either Como or Bournemouth. United’s last two matches have brought a victory and a draw. They have also conceded nine in the last three. The faith Wilcox, Berrada – and, by extension, Sir Jim Ratcliffe and the Glazers – showed in Carrick has been rewarded in parts of the games against Chelsea, Roma and Aston Villa, but certainly not over the full 90 minutes of any. Sunday’s 4-4 draw against Villa, following Tuesday’s 3-2 win over Roma, seemed to underline that United are not a serious football team any more. Indeed, ragged and ramshackle, there are times when they are barely a team at all.

And yet, with Carrick imperilled, they have summoned something from within, twice in five days. Maybe they are playing to save the manager’s skin.

But the precedents also included United’s previous 4-4 draw, 11 months ago against Bournemouth and in the final weeks of Ruben Amorim’s reign. Were they playing for him, or just in a game that took on a life of its own? There was also the example of Amorim’s 5-4 win over Lyon; like Carrick’s triumph over Roma, the craziness of it was scarcely a blueprint for future success.

So Wilcox and Berrada have much to ponder. When they opted not to sack Carrick two weeks ago, perhaps it was a case of timing, that their succession planning was not sufficiently advanced to move. They may have been put off by the Fletcher precedent: United’s former players tended to have excellent records as caretaker managers until Darren Fletcher found it tougher in January. Wilcox and Berrada might have found themselves without an ideal interim or permanent replacement.

Now an international break provides the chance to make a change. The onus switches to United’s powerbrokers, even if they again opt not to act. Carrick has confirmed he will not quit. “That’s not going to happen,” he said. Sacking him could make unpopular figures even more unpopular; or it could save the crowd from turning on an Old Trafford favourite. In the triangle of players, manager and hierarchy, only Carrick has had the vocal backing of the Stretford End of late. Carrick may have credit in the bank with the money men as well: by transforming last season and taking United into the Champions League, he made the club £100m.

But he has also taken them into the relegation zone in November. In itself, that could be deemed a sackable offence. Indeed, United have made their worst start in 40 years, when they also took eight points from their first 10 games, when they sacked their manager in November. But Ron Atkinson left them one place above the bottom three; they dropped into it after Sir Alex Ferguson’s first game, a 2-0 defeat at Oxford.

United even had more league wins at this stage in 1973-74, in the season when they went on to be relegated, when, at Christmas, their joint top scorer was the goalkeeper, Alex Stepney.

And for now, United are in danger of the drop. When Amorim talked of them being in a relegation battle in December 2024, they were not really; they had enough of a cushion. Not now; while December could offer some respite, containing meetings with the rest of the current bottom five, the risk is that United’s plight worsens before then.

There is no quick fix, no easy answers. Maybe United’s dismal defence will have more solidity when Luke Shaw is finally fit again. There was certainly the sense that the returning players could prove to be reinforcements. The fit-again Dorgu, who scored in Carrick’s first two games in January, might have scored in his last or extended his tenure. There are signs that, after so many were out of form, a couple of Carrick’s squad are belatedly showing something resembling their best, as Matheus Cunha has in the last two matches. But there are others who have got still worse.

So United need someone to transform their season in the way Carrick himself did last year, even if it is only the temporary boost a new-manager bounce can provide. But they can’t sack Michael Carrick and then appoint Michael Carrick. Do Wilcox and Berrada stick or twist? If the outside world does not know, perhaps they don’t either.

Can weight-loss drugs protect the brain? The evidence is complicated

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A new review examines whether GLP-1 weight-loss drugs could preserve memory and why their potential benefits for brain health remain uncertain.

Semaglutide can help people lose weight and lower cardiovascular risk. But can it also help preserve memory and thinking skills?

That question sits at the center of a new review covering nearly 200 preclinical studies and some of the largest clinical trials ever run on these medications.

The short version: maybe. The honest version: we still don’t know how, or even if.

The review was led by Stina Börchers and Christoffer Clemmensen at the University of Copenhagen. What they’ve assembled is part map, part warning label.

Look at population-level studies and the picture is seductive.

Trawl through insurance databases and clinical registries and you’ll find that people with type 2 diabetes taking GLP-1 receptor agonists consistently develop dementia at lower rates than those on other glucose-lowering therapies.

The REWIND trial flagged a 14% lower hazard of substantial cognitive decline with dulaglutide. Headlines practically write themselves.

Cognition was an exploratory outcome in REWIND. The 14% reduction emerged after a post hoc adjustment for participants’ baseline cognitive scores.

A meta-analysis pooling five randomized clinical trials, 7,732 adults with type 2 diabetes in total, found no clear improvement in global cognitive performance.

The observational findings remain promising, but randomized trials have not established a clear cognitive benefit.

The sharpest disappointments came from Alzheimer’s research. ELAD tested liraglutide in 204 people with mild-to-moderate Alzheimer’s for a year.

It missed its primary endpoint, which measured brain glucose metabolism.

Some MRI measures and a secondary cognitive outcome favored liraglutide, but measures of daily functioning and overall clinical severity did not show significant benefits.

EVOKE and EVOKE+ went much bigger. These phase 3 trials enrolled 3,808 participants with mild cognitive impairment or early Alzheimer’s.

Participants were assigned to oral semaglutide, with a target dose of 14 mg daily, or placebo for two years.

The drug didn’t slow decline on the Clinical Dementia Rating scale and it didn’t help with daily functioning.

It did nudge some biomarkers, though – roughly 10% reductions in cerebrospinal fluid levels of phosphorylated tau and neurogranin.

But biomarker movement, by itself, is not evidence that a disease is being modified.

GLP-1 receptors are sprinkled across brain regions you’d want a cognition drug to reach – hippocampus, amygdala, prefrontal cortex, substantia nigra.

Preclinical work shows effects on synaptic plasticity and neurotrophic signaling through CREB and BDNF pathways. On paper, the biology is there.

In practice, these drugs are large peptide molecules. They don’t cross the blood-brain barrier easily.

Most evidence suggests they reach circumventricular organs – spots where capillaries are naturally leaky – and parts of the brainstem.

Whether these drugs ever touch the hippocampus in pharmacologically relevant concentrations depends on the compound, the species, the dose, and the detection method. Nobody has a clean answer.

This raises a question about GLP-1 drugs and the brain: Could any cognitive benefits come indirectly from better metabolic health?

They could be side effects of side effects – downstream consequences of tighter blood sugar, less inflammation, healthier blood vessels, better sleep.

Semaglutide cut cardiovascular events by 20% in the SELECT trial. It reduced major kidney outcomes by 24% in FLOW.

Tirzepatide dramatically improved obstructive sleep apnea in the SURMOUNT-OSA trials. Every one of those improvements could benefit the brain indirectly.

“We need to find out whether these treatments can help people with obesity or diabetes preserve their cognitive abilities, particularly if treatment begins before cognitive problems become established. It is also important to identify which patients might benefit,” Börchers said.

Obesity and diabetes can affect attention, planning, and processing speed.

Alzheimer’s disease often affects the ability to remember events and experiences early in its course. These patterns can overlap, but their underlying causes and responses to treatment may differ.

These are not the same problem dressed in different clothes. They arise from different biology.

A drug that might improve executive function in someone with metabolic syndrome is not necessarily going to rescue memory in someone with amyloid plaques.

Worse, the relationship between blood sugar and cognition runs in both directions.

Hypoglycemia damages thinking. But cognitive impairment also makes dangerous blood sugar crashes more likely. Picking apart cause and effect inside that loop is a nightmare.

Rapid weight loss can reduce muscle mass, potentially worsening frailty in vulnerable older adults.

It can cause dehydration, malnutrition, and reduced protein intake. Fatigue shows up more often with incretin-based therapies than with placebo.

In people already teetering toward frailty, these side effects could erase whatever cognitive benefit the metabolic improvements might deliver.

The review calls for trials that track body composition, nutritional status, and treatment tolerability alongside cognition.

Newer treatments combine GLP-1 activity with effects on GIP, glucagon, or amylin pathways. Different combinations may have different effects on brain function.

GIPR agonism and antagonism, for instance, appear to activate entirely different brain mechanisms rather than simply opposing each other.

Brain-penetrant peptides and small-molecule agonists could eventually bypass the blood-brain barrier problem altogether.

The OxSENSE study is already testing semaglutide’s acute effects on reward sensitivity, memory, and emotional processing in healthy adults.

“Obesity medicines are becoming increasingly sophisticated, offering new opportunities to understand how metabolic regulation influences brain function,” Clemmensen said.

“By disentangling the direct and indirect effects of these drugs on the brain, we may ultimately be able to develop treatments specifically aimed at preserving cognitive function.”

The review is published in the journal Science.

São Paulo Grand Prix Proves F1 Can Still Thrill In The Rain

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Rain is often called the great equalizer in Formula 1, with slippery conditions making driver ability far more crucial to success than car performance. F1 rekindled its wet-weather magic on Sunday in São Paulo, Brazil. As the dominant Mercedes team faltered, 2025 champion Lando Norris showed poise through changing conditions to take his third victory this season. The weekend at Interlagos broke the recent trend of rain halting on-track action instead of shaking up the competitive order. Actually racing in the rain had become as rare as spotting a dodo bird in the wild.

Saturday previewed both sides of the wet-weather coin. After a heavy downpour delayed Q1 by more than an hour, fans were treated to one of the season's best qualifying sessions. Wily veterans Fernando Alonso and Nico Hülkenberg dragged their subpar equipment into Q3. They were joined in the final segment by Red Bull's often-scrutinized prospects Liam Lawson and Isack Hadjar. In all, nine teams slotted into Q3's 10 spots.

Total frustration for George 😖#F1 #BrazilGP pic.twitter.com/yb6vJsOzOx

An approaching storm cut the fight for pole into a one-lap shootout. Norris put his McLaren on provisional pole, and George Russell hoped to beat the lap time. However, the Mercedes driver was impeded by Williams' Alex Albon and reassured by his team that he could squeeze in a second lap. When heavy showers proved another run was impossible, Russell lashed out over the radio. Team principal Toto Wolff had to chime in to calm him down.

Norris wasn't as fortunate at the start on Sunday. With the track still wet from earlier rain, the field took to the grid on intermediate tires. Mercedes' Kimi Antonelli actually slid off the track on his reconnaissance lap. The new world champion would have much better traction from his second-place grid box. He launched past the McLaren for the lead into Turn 1 with Lawson following him into second. More action unfolded behind the leaders. Despite starting 20th, Lewis Hamilton flew through the field and was ninth by the end of the opening lap. Max Verstappen made it to 11th from 21st.

Watch the seven-time world champ charge through the field at the start 🤩#F1 #BrazilGP pic.twitter.com/M685ysLctw

F1's phenoms didn't just leverage their wet-weather prowess, but also adapted better to the changing conditions. After prodding the Ferrari pit wall, Hamilton was the first driver to switch to slicks on lap 14 of the 71-lap contest. The seven-time champion, making his 400th career start, was immediately the fastest driver on track. With a dry line forming, the challenge was getting the tire up to the optimum temperature on the cool track. Russell spun in the pit exit after his stop. Others ran wide into the runoff areas in corners.

Here's Antonelli's spin at Turn 1 which handed Norris the lead 👀#F1 #BrazilGP pic.twitter.com/bZRcgm07h5

The biggest casualty was Antonelli, who lost the lead to Norris on lap 19 after spinning in Turn 1. He had reported a strange issue where his Mercedes was pulling one side under acceleration. The issue would also crop up on Russell's car. The rain would return with 10 laps to go, but Norris safely swapped back to inters and cruised to the finish line. The only thing that went wrong for Norris was him shattering his champagne bottle on the podium.

While Mercedes clinched the World Constructors' Championship at Interlagos, the factory team didn't have a weekend befitting a title-winning campaign. The errors behind the wheel and in the garage were impossible to ignore, even though Russell and Antonelli finished fourth and fifth, respectively. After the race, the team had no idea what went wrong with either car. Russell shared his thoughts with The Race, stating:

Maybe it's a small theory because we were the first ones onto the inters, even just on that outlap on the driest track, you damage the tires and the temps go up and you're in this negative spiral. As of now, that's the only explanation."

The unpredictability of wet races is always thrilling. It's a shame we can't see racing in the rain more often. The São Paulo Grand Prix thankfully fell within a golden window where the track could be wet without the rooster tails reducing visibility to effectively nothing. The FIA trialed wheel covers in 2024 to reduce the spray, but they had no impact. The reasoning behind them ignored that the car's floor is an aerodynamic device that sucks in water and shoots it out of the rear diffuser. The only guaranteed solution would be to somehow slow the cars down, which would be universally unpopular.

‘Maxton Hall – The World Between Us’: James Fights For Ruby In Trailer For Final Season Of Prime Video YA Drama

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

In the trailer for the final season of Maxton Hall – The World Between Us, Ruby’s (Harriet Herbig-Matten) future hangs by a thread, but James (Damian Hardung) isn’t about to let her life explode.

The Prime Video original from Germany, whose third season premieres on December 9, follows the rollercoaster relationship between Ruby and James at an elite English private school. Though they’ve run into many problems in the past, the trailer sees James tell an expelled Ruby, “I will not give up until you’re back at Maxton Hall.”

The setup for final season sees Ruby banned from the college and her dream of going to Oxford University slipping away. Together with James, they fight to prove her innocence, but the closer they get to the truth, the more it threatens to tear everything apart. The trailer begins with an emotional Ruby saying, “And so now I’ve been expelled,” over an image of her with a teacher, which has been edited to make them appear in a compromising position. Lexington (Thomas Douglas), the acting director of Maxton Hall, then tells her, “Oxford, you can forget it.” Watch on Deadline

The third season of the series, which comes from Fremantle’s UFA Fiction, is based on Save Us, the third installment of the bestselling trilogy by author Mona Kasten.

The German-language show stars Herbig-Matten and Hardung, along with Sonja Weißer as Lydia, Eidin Jalali as Graham, Ben Felipe as Cyril, Fedja van Huêt as Mortimer, Runa Greiner as Ember, Justus Riesner as Alistair, Govinda Gabriel as Kesh, Andrea Guo as Lin, Frederic Balonier as Kieran, Eli Riccardi as Elaine and Dagny Dewath as Ophelia.

Martin Schreier is the director with the exec producers being Markus Brunnemann, Eike Adler, and Ceylan Yildirim, who is also the head writer of the series. Sandra Stöckmann serves as head writer alongside Yildirum, with additional writers being Catharina Junk, Marlene Melchior and Aylin Kockler. Valentin Debler returns as producer. The series is supported by the German Motion Picture Fund.

The first two seasons of the show were huge hits for Amazon and its streaming service, with Season 2 hit the number one spot in 42 markets including the U.S., UK, Germany, France, Australia, Italy, Spain and Canada.

The first two episodes of the six-part Season 3 will launch on Prime Video on December 9 with the finale dropping on December 23.

What happens when engineers grind up eggshells and squeeze them into a lightweight metal?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Eggshell powder pressed into magnesium bonded to the metal and made tiny samples tougher to crush.

Most eggshells end up in the trash or the compost bin. Yet a shell is almost pure calcium carbonate, the same calcium compound that metal makers normally get from mined rock.

Engineers have now packed ground-up kitchen eggshells into a block of magnesium metal and pressed it through a spinning die. The shell powder spread evenly through the metal.

Part of it also reacted with the magnesium around it, which bonded shell to metal chemically. That makes a kitchen scrap a workable ingredient for a lightweight metal, at least in a small lab test.

The work came from the lab of Bharat Gwalani, an assistant professor of materials science and engineering at North Carolina State University.

It was a first test on small rods, and the finished metal wasn’t measurably harder than plain magnesium. Its gain in strength showed up only in microscopic crush tests.

Magnesium is a light metal. A recent antenna design for Mars helicopters used a magnesium frame.

“Calcium is added to magnesium to improve its mechanical properties,” Gwalani said. “We wanted to see if we could use biogenic waste – eggshells – to produce the necessary calcium materials during the manufacturing process.”

Biogenic waste just means leftovers from living things. Eggshells already appear on lists of food leftovers that could be recovered and reused, and other labs have turned food waste into plastic.

Industry makes one of those compounds, calcium oxide, by heating limestone in industrial kilns until it gives off carbon dioxide.

Earlier teams had already blended eggshell powder into magnesium, either by melting the metal or by stirring the powder into its surface.

However, little was known about what happens to the shell inside the metal during a friction-based press like this one.

Gwalani’s co-authors included researchers at Pacific Northwest National Laboratory, where first author Aniruddha Malakar now works, and at the Indian Institute of Technology Delhi.

The eggshells came from ordinary households. The team washed them, dried them in an oven at about 300°F (150°C) for four hours, and ground them with a mortar and pestle.

Then they cut small grooves, each about an eighth of an inch (3 mm) wide, into a short magnesium cylinder about an inch (25.4 mm) across. The eggshell powder filled the grooves in the metal and made up roughly 8.4 percent of the block by volume.

That’s a pinch of shell in a lot of metal.

A die spinning 300 times a minute pressed into the block at about 3 inches (75 mm) a minute and squeezed it out as a rod. Friction and squeezing heated the metal to roughly 930°F (500°C), hot enough to soften it without melting it.

Most of the shell particles measured 2 to 4 micrometers across, far thinner than a human hair. They were spread evenly through the rod.

Metal is made of tiny crystals called grains. In a plain magnesium rod made the same way, the grains averaged 16 micrometers across. With eggshell mixed in, they averaged 8.

Smaller grains usually make a metal stronger. Even so, the eggshell rod wasn’t measurably harder than the plain one when the team pressed a diamond tip into its surface.

The eggshell’s effect appeared only after the metal started to give way. The researchers carved microscopic pillars out of grains pointing in the same direction in both metals. Then they crushed them.

Both kinds of pillar began to give way at about the same stress. Once they did, the eggshell pillars kept resisting harder as they squashed.

Their peak strength reached about 32,600 pounds per square inch (225 megapascals), compared with about 29,000 pounds per square inch (200 megapascals) for plain magnesium.

The team thinks the shell bits help tiny flaws in the metal’s crystals pile up as it deforms. The more of those flaws that build up, the harder the metal is to squash further.

The second finding was chemical. Calcium carbonate usually breaks apart into calcium oxide and carbon dioxide at about 1,650 to 1,740°F (900 to 950°C) under normal air pressure.

Inside the press, the metal reached only about 930°F (500°C). Several of the smallest shell fragments broke down, at least partly. The team established this by measuring calcium, oxygen and carbon across individual particles.

The researchers think the spots where shell met metal got much hotter than the overall reading. They suspect sudden swings in pressure helped too.

Near the edges of some particles, the measurements pointed to magnesium oxide. The likeliest source, the team concluded, was carbon dioxide from the broken-down shell reacting with the surrounding magnesium.

The reaction was patchy, though. Larger particles, a few micrometers or more across, stayed mostly unchanged. The finished rod held a mix of untouched shell, partly broken-down shell and areas rich in calcium oxide.

One compound didn’t form. That compound, Mg2Ca, is a compound of magnesium and calcium that can strengthen magnesium alloys, and the team didn’t detect it. The calcium stayed mostly locked up as calcium oxide.

The authors wrote that their goal was to understand what happens inside the metal, not to make the strongest possible material.

They tested only one spin speed and one feed rate. The team also didn’t measure whether the process uses less energy than making calcium compounds from rock, although Gwalani said it should.

“There are fewer steps,” Gwalani said. “You have a reliable, sustainable supply chain. Eggshells are inexpensive.”

Gwalani’s group in NC State’s Department of Materials Science and Engineering has also used the same kind of press to work magnetic powder into scrap aluminum.

The next step is to push the chemistry further. The authors suggested heating the shell powder before it goes in, running the press longer or hotter, or working in a sealed chamber with less oxygen.

Any of those might free the calcium from its oxide and get it into the magnesium itself, the step that this first test didn’t reach.

The full study was published in the journal Journal of Magnesium and Alloys.

Nos prêtés ne sont pas prêts (4) !

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Suite de notre série sur nos joueurs prêtés. Pour ce 4ème épisode, tandis que leurs anciens coéquipiers ont retrouvé le chemin de la victoire face à Laval, les 9 joueurs partis voir si l'herbe était plus verte ailleurs ont plus joué, mais moins gagné.

En effet on compte 5 titularisations (sur 9) mais 1 seule victoire pour 2 nuls et 5 défaites.

Du côté des championnats étrangers, le plus utilisé est toujours Igor Miladinovic. Il a vécu sa 11e titularisation en championnat ce week-end pour une défaite à domicile (1-2) dans un duel de mal classés. Il totalise désormais 879 minutes de jeu (plus 113 en Coupe d’Europe) et le Partizan Belgrade glisse à la 10e place sur 14 après 15 journées.

Son voisin Strahinja Stojkovic, de nouveau titulaire avec l’OFK Belgrade a encore été battu (2-1) à l’extérieur. Désormais 12e du championnat serbe et aux portes de la zone rouge notre latéral droit peut néanmoins se réjouir d’avoir connu sa 4e titularisation. Il compte désormais 296 minutes de jeu

En Roumanie, la situation s’enlise pour Ebenezer Annan. Le Rapid Bucarest a fait match nul (1-1), et reste 3e au classement, avec Annan resté sur le banc. Sa dernière titularisation (la 3e) remonte au 18 septembre, et il totalise 241 minutes en championnat.

Direction l’Espagne ou Lucas Stassin peut se réjouir de sa nouvelle titularisation face à Alavès. Mais il n’a pu faire que match nul (0-0). Il compte désormais 472 minutes de jeu en Liga en 7 matchs (5 titularisations et 2 sorties de banc) et a marqué 1 but et délivré 1 passe décisive.

En d2 espagnole, comme le week-end précédent, Aïmen Moueffek était titulaire au milieu de terrain, mais comme le week-end précédent, il est sorti à le mi-temps. Et son club de Cadix s’est incliné (1-2) à domicile face à Oviedo.  S’il totalise désormais 202 minutes de jeu en championnat (3 titularisations) et 45 en coupe, Moueffek reste enlisé dans la zone rouge avec une 19e place (sur 22) après 13 journées.

Dans l’hexagone, on note une éclaircie bourguignonne : En effet, si Djylian N’Guessan a battu sans transpirer depuis son banc les Vilains (2-0) avec Brest et reste scotché à 28 minutes de jeu depuis le début de saison, Pierre Ekwah a connu sa première titularisation face au PFC samedi pour une défaite à la clé (0-1), mais une prestation jugée satisfaisante par son coach, et un temps de jeu qui a triplé (142 minutes depuis le début de saison).

A Pau en revanche, pas d’éclaircie, Luan Gadegbeku n’était pas dans le groupe défait par Nantes (0-2), mais en a pris 3 à Trelissac avec l’équipe réserve et reste sur son maigre total de 147 minutes de jeu. Axel Dodote était, lui, sur le banc, mais n’en a pas bougé, et reste sur ses 472 minutes de jeu estivales. L’expulsion d’Anthony Briançon lui offrira-t-il l’occasion de regoûter au terrain ?

Two EFL club takeovers collapse at final hour

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Both AFC Wimbledon's and Bromley's proposed investment plans have hit a roadblock at the eleventh hour.

The League One duo were expected to struggle in the third tier this year. Bromley are in the division for the first time in their history, having only just been promoted into the EFL for the first time three years ago, while AFC Wimbledon are proudly fan-owned.

Ultimately, with the finances in football only increasing, plus some of the juggernauts in the division this year, such as Leicester City, Huddersfield Town and Sheffield Wednesday, who can afford to invest in the squad and the infrastructure at the club, their backs were always going to be against the wall.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

It seemed as if they'd be given some financial help in the not-so-distant future, though. AFC Wimbledon were approached by a consortium led by ex-Chelsea defender Ryan Bertrand to take a minority 34% of the club's shares, while a consortium led by former Everton frontman Victor Anichebe was heavily linked with a takeover at Hayes Lane.

However, in recent updates, AFC Wimbledon have confirmed that the Sloane Street Capital investment group has withdrawn from their deal to invest up to £87 million over five years, one week ahead of what would have been an important fan vote to pass it.

Bertrand needed the financial backing of British technology expert Omar Mir to go through with the minority investment, but his 'investment priorities have changed,' according to a statement.

The collapse hasn't deterred the 37-year-old ex-England international, though, with it said that he, alongside his partners at Sloane Street Football, will be seeking fresh investment and returning to the Dons with a proposal on the same terms.

Meanwhile, Pete O'Rourke has reported that the African consortium's takeover of Bromley, fronted by Anichebe, is on the verge of collapse, which, to some, can be seen as a bullet dodged.

The Shoreline Limited group has been the main avenue of blame attributed to the state that National League side Gateshead are currently in.

They attempted to buy the North East club a couple of seasons ago, but after it failed, the group needed to sign waivers over money they claim they are owed, so the side currently at the bottom of the fifth tier can be taken over by another group and avoid the possibility of liquidation.

While not a concrete fact, it's strongly inferred that Andy Woodman's dismissal after five-and-a-half years in charge of Bromley, during which he won the FA Trophy and two promotions to the highest level the Ravens have played at, was down to the proposed takeover.

Many feel that the sacking of Woodman just 10 games into the League One campaign was in favour of a new brand of football that this new consortium wanted to see at Hayes Lane. Now, some feel that an unpopular dismissal was for nothing.

One fan claimed that the group had "turned Gateshead into near collapse," and "ruined 2 football clubs," after this breakdown.

Some are now worrying that, as with Gateshead, Shoreline Sports could end up suing the club over financial claims against shareholders, which could put any future investment into the club in jeopardy.

Ultimately, it's been quite a mess at Bromley in the opening stages of the season. Karl Robinson lost his first game in charge in the FA Cup against a Rotherham United side experiencing troubling times themselves in League Two.

This failed takeover will just create more confusion and uncertainty about the club's future, which will detract from what was set to be a historic campaign in the club's history.

In an ideal world, AFC Wimbledon wouldn't need any minority investment, and they'd be able to compete solely as a fan-run club.

The reality isn't that, though, and it's something that the Dons fans have come to accept if they are to break through the glass ceiling currently encasing their ambitions.

Do you think either AFC Wimbledon or Bromley will receive some investment before the end of 2026? Let us know in the comments.

As long as any funding doesn't turn into a full-blown takeover, that's the compromise that a club like AFC Wimbledon has to take, and this proposed 34% investment wouldn't have done so. Therefore, some Dons fans were actually looking forward to getting it over the line.

The news surrounding SSC pulling out is a disappointment, but there can be some optimism in the fact that Bertrand and co are still seeking investment routes into the club. Now all they have to do is wait to see if that comes.

Meanwhile, both sides will continue to punch above their weight and battle bravely against the third-tier drop this season.

Tesla launches Robotaxi in San Antonio with a 274 sq mi geofence on paper

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Tesla is launching its Robotaxi service in San Antonio, its eighth city, with a launch geofence covering about 274 square miles. That’s the biggest day-one map Tesla has ever posted, and more than four times the size of Waymo’s San Antonio service area.

But if Tesla’s recent maps are any guide, a big chunk of that area won’t be bookable. In Austin, an independent check found the Robotaxi app only accepted pickups and drop-offs in about 59% of the zone Tesla advertises.

Tesla’s official Robotaxi account made the announcement just after midnight local time: “Robotaxi available in San Antonio starting tomorrow, 5am.” It came with a map of the “launch geofence,” a large polygon that runs from Loop 1604 on the north side down to just south of downtown.

Robotaxi Tracker, which measures operator maps and logs fleet sightings, puts the zone at 274 square miles. That covers about 1.0 million residents (51% of the metro) and 721,000 jobs. San Antonio International Airport sits inside the polygon, but it isn’t served.

Waymo started driverless rides in San Antonio in February with more than 60 square miles and opened to all riders in October, airport trips included. Robotaxi Tracker currently lists Waymo’s San Antonio area at 59 square miles.

What Tesla didn’t say is how many cars will cover its 274 square miles. As of Monday morning, Robotaxi Tracker hadn’t logged a single Tesla Robotaxi in San Antonio.

The launch landed the same day Tesla expanded Austin again. The tracker now measures Tesla’s Austin zone at 443 square miles, up 178 square miles since mid-September.

We’ve seen this movie. Last month, Tesla expanded its Houston geofence into the shape of a rocket, and within two days the creator of Robotaxi Tracker found the app refusing downtown pickups inside it.

Since then, Koby Pierce, who tracks Tesla’s service on X, has done the tedious work of checking city after city. He searched thousands of real addresses in the Robotaxi app on an emulator and mapped only the ones where Tesla allows a pickup or drop-off, then compared that with the map Tesla advertises. Here’s the share of Tesla’s advertised land area that actually works:

Austin's dropoff/pickup Geofence is ~41% smaller than advertised.Seems mostly the East side of town is inaccessible. Unsure the cause. Any ideas?Dallas is next and should be done by noon tomrrow. pic.twitter.com/WvU99sJlJh

Austin is the one that matters. It’s Tesla’s flagship market, with 453 unsupervised vehicles in service according to Robotaxi Tracker. Yet about 41% of the advertised map Pierce tested, mostly on the east side of town, wouldn’t take a ride request. Pierce points to railroad crossings as a big factor in several cities, which lines up with the strange downtown routing we saw in Houston.

To be fair, Pierce says Waymo is next on his list, and he expects “they’re no better.” Robotaxi Tracker already had to trim Waymo’s Orlando area last month after finding the airport, inside Waymo’s published map, wasn’t selectable. We’ll look at those numbers when they’re out. But it doesn’t change what Tesla is doing with San Antonio today.

Credit where it’s due. A 274-square-mile launch in a new city is progress, and Tesla now runs unsupervised rides in eight cities. At the start of this year, it had no unsupervised rides anywhere.

But we need to stop taking Tesla’s geofence numbers at face value, and that includes us. We used Tesla’s 254-square-mile figure for Houston, and it turned out to be overstated. In all six cities Pierce has checked, the real service area is between 59% and 78% of what Tesla posts. In Austin and Miami, it’s about 60%.

So the honest way to read the San Antonio announcement is “up to 274 square miles.” In practice, expect something closer to 160 to 210 square miles, with an unknown number of cars, and the airport already off the table. That’s still bigger than Waymo’s 59 square miles in the city.

The problem is that square miles are the metric Tesla fully controls. Drawing a polygon costs nothing, and the numbers were all over X within hours of the post. Cars per square mile, wait times, and rides completed are much harder to inflate, and nationally Waymo still runs over 4,000 driverless vehicles to Tesla’s 666, per Robotaxi Tracker.

I still consider this a small pilot by any standard, and safety remains the bottleneck. Tesla is still forcing riders through hour-long rides that should take 15 minutes because they have to take routes that avoid areas that Tesla can’t safely navigate without supervision.

Until that’s fixed, robotaxi won’t be able to meaningfully scale beyond these limited demonstrations in geo-fenced areas.

Robotaxi or not, most EV charging still happens at home, and pairing it with rooftop solar can cut your cost per mile to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. From paper routes to fighter jets: Shield AI’s bet on Seattle is a homecoming for its co-founders

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop & John Cook on Nov 9, 2026 at 6:36 amNovember 9, 2026 at 6:36 am

As a kid on Mercer Island, Brandon Tseng delivered The Seattle Times and the Mercer Island Reporter for extra cash. Now he and his older brother, Ryan Tseng, are making news of their own in the Seattle region.

Shield AI, the San Diego defense tech company the brothers co-founded in 2015, announced a deal last week to buy Kirkland, Wash.-based radar maker Echodyne for up to $5.3 billion. Shield, now valued at $28.8 billion, had already unveiled plans in September to build its AI-piloted X-BAT fighter jet at a factory south of Seattle.

“I believe we will become one of the largest, or at least the fastest-growing employer in this region over the next several years,” Brandon Tseng said in an interview with GeekWire in the hours after the Echodyne acquisition was announced.

The company projects that it will employ about 4,000 people in the Seattle area by the early 2030s. It estimates the X-BAT could support about 8,000 jobs across the region in all, including jobs at Shield and its suppliers, according to an economic analysis drawing on company figures.

Tseng said Shield expects to have “well north of a million square feet” in the area before long, up from its initial 420,000-square-foot facility in Des Moines, Wash.

“That will really just be the beginning,” he said.

Brandon Tseng said the biggest reason Shield chose the Pacific Northwest for X-BAT production was the region’s “density of engineering and manufacturing talent,” pointing to Boeing, Microsoft and Amazon.

Inside the Echodyne deal: Shield believes the defense tech companies that succeed will need products that “sense, think, and act,” Tseng said. Its Hivemind autonomy software handles the thinking, and its V-BAT drone and X-BAT jet will handle the acting.

Echodyne becomes “the cornerstone of the sensing piece,” he said.

Tseng said Shield got a closer look at the market for sensors when it bought Australian optics company Sentient Vision Systems in 2024. That led the company to start looking seriously at other sensor makers, and eventually to Echodyne.

Shield and Echodyne had first crossed paths in 2016, when both were fledgling companies in the Department of Homeland Security’s Silicon Valley Innovation Program.

Nearly a decade later, the two companies’ teams were talking about working together when Echodyne began exploring a sale, Tseng said. The deal came together over several months. Tseng was at Echodyne the day it was announced last week, as the companies held all-hands meetings with their teams.

It’s an unusual deal: one venture-backed startup buying another, funded in part by a new venture round. Shield is raising $2.5 billion to help pay for the deal, including $1 billion in new funding at the $28.8 billion valuation — more than double what it was worth in March.

Echodyne has about 400 employees, after opening a $40 million radar factory in Woodinville in July. No layoffs are planned, the companies said. Eben Frankenberg, Echodyne’s co-founder and CEO, will stay in that role, reporting to Shield CEO Gary Steele. The combined company will have about 2,700 employees.

The deal is expected to close in early 2027, pending regulatory approval. Tseng said Shield plans a light touch for roughly the first year after that. Echodyne will keep selling radar to other manufacturers, many of them in counter-drone systems, while pursuing performance targets that could add $500 million to the price.

Deeper product integrations will be announced later, he said.

“They’ve been on a tear growth-wise, and we don’t want to get in their way,” Tseng said. “We don’t want to slow them down. We just want to supercharge them.”

He called the two companies’ cultures a strong match, describing both as mission-oriented and engineering-first. Echodyne’s best-selling radar, EchoShield, is designed to protect U.S. and allied service members from incoming drones.

Echodyne spun out of Intellectual Ventures, the Bellevue invention lab, in 2014 with a $15 million round led by Bill Gates and Madrona. Madrona and other Echodyne investors will become significant Shield shareholders.

Tim Porter, the Madrona Venture Group managing director who has served on Echodyne’s board since 2014, called the deal another sign of the defense tech industry’s rapid growth in the Pacific Northwest.

Echodyne “rarely, if ever, showed up on lists of the hottest defense tech companies,” he wrote. Now it’s “under the radar no longer.”

X-BAT bet: Much of Shield’s growth in the region depends on an aircraft that hasn’t flown yet. Shield unveiled its first full-scale X-BAT prototype this month and shipped it to Kansas for flight testing, with first flight targeted by the end of the year. Shield has said low-rate production will start in Des Moines in mid-2027, with the first locally built jet due in early 2028.

The Pentagon and Shield have committed a combined $400 million to X-BAT’s development, but Shield hasn’t announced any production orders.

Ryan Tseng, speaking to Breaking Defense on the sidelines of the International Fighter Conference in Munich last week, said Shield expects to announce European X-BAT orders and partnerships but declined to discuss quantities, saying only, “Watch this space.” Andrew Hawkes, Shield’s X-BAT director for Europe, told Breaking Defense the company has “several active” sales campaigns there but declined to name customers.

Asked about the X-BAT schedule in the interview last week, Brandon Tseng acknowledged the challenge but pointed to the engineering and manufacturing teams that brought the prototype together in about a year. “There was no quit in them,” he said.

“It’s hard as hell, right? Just to move out on production that fast, but you have to be confident,” he said. “I’m confident.”

Tseng, a Naval Academy graduate who deployed twice to Afghanistan as a Navy SEAL, compared Shield’s pace to that experience, when small, mission-driven groups with the right tools and resources would “do things that people didn’t think were possible.”

Ryan Tseng, an electrical engineer, previously founded wireless charging company WiPower, which was acquired by Qualcomm. The brothers started Shield with Andrew Reiter, Shield’s first CTO, who returned to the company this year.

Seattle roots: The Tseng brothers and their sister grew up on Mercer Island. Brandon Tseng spent summers swimming in Lake Washington and played water polo for Mercer Island. Ryan Tseng, who was Shield’s CEO for about a decade, graduated from Mercer Island High School, and their sister graduated from the University of Washington.

Their father, Carlos Tseng, was CEO of Seattle-based Foss Maritime from 1996 to 2001, according to Tseng and Shield. He would stop at Pike Place Market on the way home from work, and the family “probably ate salmon every other day,” Brandon Tseng said.

The family moved to Florida in 2001. None of the Tsengs live in the area anymore, but Brandon Tseng still has friends here from middle school and high school and stops by Mercer Island when he has time on his visits to the region.

Shield’s Washington state connections go further. Steele, the company’s CEO since May 2025, grew up in Yakima and graduated from Washington State University.

Brandon Tseng called Shield’s expansion in the region “a fun homecoming” for him, his brother and Steele. He is based in Washington, D.C., but expects to spend more and more time in the Seattle area.

Tseng delivered the Times, then the afternoon paper, seven days a week from age 10 to 14, making his rounds every day after school and on weekends. He credits those routes with teaching him about hard work.

“It’s always felt like home,” he said. “It’s great to be back here.”

Nintendo's legendary Kirby designer Masahiro Sakurai weighs in on gen-AI, saying "it has yet to gain my trust" but not to reject it completely

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

"Doing nothing is probably not the right approach."

Masahiro Sakurai, the designer behind many iconic Nintendo franchises such as Super Smash Bros. and several Kirby titles, has weighed in on the ongoing discussion of generative-AI use in game development.

At an event celebrating the release of his new book - Masahiro Sakurai’s Thoughts on Games - the veteran director told Famitsu he hasn't and currently isn't using the technology to make games, noting that the role of a designer is to generate ideas.

"It has yet to gain my trust," Sakurai said via DeepL machine translation. Regarding graphical use specifically, he added that, "It must not amount to exploitation of any kind," and that "for the time being, we can produce better results by coming up with ideas ourselves".

But Sakurai is wary of rejecting gen-AI completely, saying "making games today is far too difficult", with some games "running at a loss even after selling millions of copies". A company in that position may go under if it does not change its stance on generative-AI, he suggested. "Doing nothing is probably not the right approach," he said, but "where and how to use it" is a complex topic with a lot of uncertainties. Sakurai is taking "a slow-paced approach" for now.

Outside of regular industry layoffs, the use of generative-AI in games has been the most controversial topic amongst developers and players. Some argue it's already being used in multiple development disciplines, notably programming and writing code. Others, such as Shigeru Miyamoto, are more sceptical of the technology's capabilities, especially when it comes to the more creative and art-driven parts of game development.

Coinbase’s blockchain got faster. Some apps can still connect but get no data

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Apps still using Base’s retired Flashblocks feeds can connect without receiving updates. They need a new data route, not another reconnect.

Coinbase’s Base blockchain retired its Flashblocks data feeds in the Nov. 5 Denim upgrade, requiring apps using them to migrate. The old WebSocket endpoints still accept connections but no longer deliver data, according to Base’s migration instructions.

Denim activated on mainnet at 18:00 UTC on Nov. 5. Base’s upgrade overview describes complete canonical blocks every 200 milliseconds, each with its own hash, state root and receipts. These are the chain’s blocks rather than previews of a block still being built.

The compatibility trap is that an open connection no longer establishes that an app is receiving updates. Reconnecting to a retired Flashblocks endpoint leaves the underlying integration problem unresolved.

Denim retired Flashblocks. Apps must move their data route to canonical block and RPC streams.

Replace the Flashblocks data route and review what pending-state requests now return.

Schematic routes, not measured traffic or transaction-inclusion latency. Canonical block inclusion remains separate from Ethereum finality.

Schematic data routes, not a traffic measurement or latency scale. Rectangles represent complete canonical blocks; arrows identify the replacement data route, with no implied transaction-inclusion guarantee.

Schematic routes, not measured traffic or transaction-inclusion latency. Canonical block inclusion remains separate from Ethereum finality.

Base directs developers to replace Flashblocks connections with subscriptions through a WebSocket-capable RPC provider or their own node. Its replacement map has three main subscription changes:

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Developers must also review what their apps expect from the pending block tag. Base still accepts that tag, but it now resolves to the latest canonical block. It no longer exposes Flashblocks preconfirmation state, and there is no direct equivalent for that former preview.

That change matters for requests reading balances, transaction counts or simulated contract results against pending state. An application retaining the old assumption can mistake an accepted response for a preview even though it is reading canonical state.

Transaction handling changes too. Apps can keep polling eth_getTransactionReceipt, but a receipt becomes available once the transaction enters a canonical 200ms block. Preconfirmed receipts and preconfirmed transaction results are no longer available.

Base recommends inventorying Flashblocks subscriptions, WebSocket connections and pending-tag requests, along with flashblock_index usage and node flags. It advises testing migrated integrations on Base Sepolia or Vibenet.

Base’s Denim upgrade notice, still marked “Verifying” on Nov. 9, carried a Nov. 5 update at 18:14 UTC reporting an investigation into periodic transaction inclusion delays and a higher than expected rate of empty blocks. The notice contained no subsequent resolution update.

A 200ms block cadence describes how often blocks arrive; it does not promise that every submitted transaction enters the next block. Inclusion also remains separate from finality. In its Denim announcement, Base says a block becomes safe when its batch is posted to Ethereum and final when Ethereum finalizes that batch. Apps needing those stronger guarantees still have to distinguish them from the arrival of a canonical block.

Russell Brand Sentenced to 10.5 Years in Prison After Rape Conviction

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Russell Brand has been handed a prison sentence of over a decade after being convicted of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault in London.

Just days after the 51-year-old was convicted of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault by a London court, he learned how much time he’ll spend behind bars.

During a court appearance on Nov. 9, judge Joel Bennathan opted to give Brand 10 years and six months in prison, according to The Telegraph. Under British law, according to the UK’s Sentencing Council, a rape conviction carries a maximum sentence of life in prison, while most sentences average around four to 19 years.

Bennathan had previously noted he planned to give Brand “significant custodial time,” per The New York Times.

While convicted on four charges, the Forgetting Sarah Marshall actor was cleared of two separate counts of rape and one count of indecent assault.

Brand’s sentencing marks the conclusion of a case that began in April 2025, when he was charged with one count of rape, one count of oral rape, two counts of sexual assault and one count of indecent assault by the London Metropolitan Police concerning events that allegedly transpired between 1999 and 2009.

Just months later, in December of that year, London police announced Brand was being charged with one additional count of rape and one more sexual assault charge over other incidents.

At the time, though he didn’t address the allegations directly beyond pleading not guilty to the charges, Brand—who shares three kids with wife Laura Brand—shared a video to social media urging viewers “to understand the truth of my situation and scenario.”

“I pray Lord, anyone that I’ve harmed or hurt in my years of mindlessness and sin would be healed, Lord,” he said in the clip. “And I pray, Lord, for the absolute truth of who I am to be abundantly revealed.”

And though Brand now faces over a decade behind bars after being convicted on some of the above charges, his legal journey, however, might not be over.

Following the verdicts on Nov. 5, the Metropolitan Police of London confirmed to E! News they were investigating two additional allegations of rape involving Brand, and that the investigation remains ongoing.

"The suspect has previously been interviewed under caution in connection with the allegations,” the statement added. "Files have been submitted to the Crown Prosecution Service in relation to both complainants. Detectives remain in close contact with prosecutors as enquiries continue and further investigative work is carried out.”

You can change Windows settings directly from a search now – how to try it

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Windows > Windows 11

The latest Windows 11 Insiders Preview enhances the Search feature so that you can access specific settings right from a search window.

Microsoft has been on a kick lately trying to improve and enhance Windows 11, including sprucing up Search, which is now simpler, cleaner, and friendlier. And there’s another cool trick that Search has up its sleeve: Available in the Beta channel of the latest Windows 11 Insiders Preview, Search will give you access to specific Windows settings and commands. Known as inline actions, this latest skill is designed to help you directly complete certain tasks. That means you no longer need to know exactly where or how to find the setting you need.

Also: Windows 11 can run better on PCs with 8GB now

Curious about built-in features such as Dark mode, Bluetooth, screen brightness, Remote Desktop, the Windows clipboard, or Windows Update? Just type the name or phrase in the Search field. The results then display an Open button that you can select to go directly to the related setting.

“A new Windows Search experience built on WinUI 3 is beginning to roll out,” Microsoft said on a web page announcing the latest Insiders build. “It’s faster, more efficient, and features a streamlined design that makes results easier to scan. Inline actions let you quickly complete tasks directly. Turn on dark mode or Bluetooth, adjust your screen brightness, mute your device, or arrange windows from Windows Search.”

I tried this with several different Windows settings, and the results window pointed me to the right screens for tweaking them. Though I know where most Windows settings are located by now, there are still times when I forget how to reach certain ones. This new skill promises to be a time saver whenever I face that challenge.

Also: 11 Windows apps I replace on every new PC

Here’s another handy enhancement. How often do you type a word or term in Windows Search only to misspell it? With the latest update, Search will now better understand what you actually mean and match you with the correct results. Type “drak” instead of “dark,” for example, and you’ll still see the results for Dark mode.

Microsoft promises these latest improvements to Windows Search will continue to be fine-tuned over the next few weeks. And there’s more in the new Preview build.

Ever run into trouble using Windows Hello facial recognition in a dark area? Now, your screen will temporarily brighten up to help you sign in under those conditions. To enable this option, head to Settings, select Account, select Sign-in options, and then click Facial recognition. Turn on the switch for “Automatically adjust screen brightness during face recognition.”

The latest Insiders build also tweaks features such as Live captions, Gaming, File Explorer, and Microsoft 365 cloud storage.

Also: How I used ChatGPT to talk me through fixing a corrupted gaming PC

To grab this update, you’ll have to join the Windows Insiders Program if you’re not already registered. You can then sign up for the Beta or Experimental channel, either of which will give you access to the new Search skills and other features. Make sure you’re running the Beta Preview Build 26320.9876 or higher. To check, go to Settings, select System, and then click About. The Windows Info section will indicate the build number.

As for regular Windows users not enrolled in the Insiders Program, the new changes should reach you relatively soon. I’d expect them to roll out with the December Patch Tuesday update arriving on December 8, so that Microsoft can squeeze them in before the end of the year.

Just when it seemed that we might be best just cancelling Christmas 2026 after the backlash against the John Lewis advert and the Dior advert, along comes Disney to save the day. The company's dropped a live-action holiday short on YouTube, and it's moving people to tears.

Ready to get into the festive spirit? Then clear the next three minutes from your calendar, saddle up and watch the film below.

The Invisible Horse (above) was directed by Dougal Wilson, the director of Paddington in Peru and features cameos from Toy Story‘s Woody and Frozen‘s Elsa.

The story is about a boy called Jack who thinks he's too old for Christmas until he discovers what he believes to be an invisible horse. That leads him to get mocked at school, but of course, things soon take a surprising turn.

The short is part of Disney's “Make the Holidays Magic” campaign, which the company says is “bringing together festive experiences, entertainment and gifting, alongside initiatives supporting children and families.”

People are welling up on YouTube, but make sure you watch the piece before reading the comments, because some of them give the twist away.

The Invisible Horse comes just a couple of weeks ahead of the release of Hexed. Given all the controversies around that movie and Disney same-face syndrome, perhaps it shows that Disney's at its most magical when it finds themes beyond witchcraft?

It just needs a powerful story. A subtle dose of nostalgia can also go a long way to reignite that feeling of childhood wonder among Disney adults.

It's enough to restore one's faith in the festive season after Kim Kardashian's shocking advent calendar.
